--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 sec: add disable_rootsquash mount option

Add the -o disable_rootsquash mount option, allowing users to
explicitly request disabling root squash on GSSIAM mounts when
authorized by the external GSSIAM authority.

Specifically, this patch implements:

- Client Mount Option:
  Adds -o disable_rootsquash parsing in lustre/llite/llite_lib.c
  and packs GSSIAM_OPT_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH in the GSSIAM init ctx
  options payload.

- Server Nodemap Privilege Raising:
  When authorized by the external GSSIAM server
  (GSSIAM_AUTH_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH), gssiam_get_nodemap() passes
  allow_root=true to nodemap_gssiam_attrs_update(). The per-identity
  dynamic child nodemap is permitted to raise allow_root via the
  parent 'gssiam' nodemap's pre-configured NODEMAP_RAISE_PRIV_ADMIN
  delegation capability. Root squash remains strictly enforced
  (allow_root=false) by default unless explicitly requested at mount
  and authorized by the GSSIAM server.

- Extended Upcall Downcall Format:
  Extends the kernel/userspace downcall contract by adding an
  ":options" field to the downcall string format parsed in
  tgt_gssiam.c ("mount_option:expire:options:principal"), allowing
  l_gssiam_auth to pass authorized permission/option flags to the
  kernel upcall cache.

- Dedicated GSSIAM Option Flags:
  Defines enum gssiam_mount_option_flags (GSSIAM_OPT_RDONLY and
  GSSIAM_OPT_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH) in lustre_idl.h, cleanly separating
  the GSSIAM option space from generic OBD_CONNECT_* connection flags.

Signed-off-by: Di Wang <ddiwang@google.com>
Change-Id: I35af55f4ff4193195028b157758a7b30bb2e0f9d

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-07-27 21:16:10.000000000) ---
Patch Set 9:

(15 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The main one is that the server treats the client's own request as the approved answer whenever the auth helper does not emit the new trailing options field, which inverts the "authorized by external GSSIAM server" model into a client-controlled one.

The new sanity-sec coverage only checks that the option string round-trips through /proc/mounts. Nothing exercises the part that matters: that a client asking for disable_rootsquash is actually squashed when the upcall does not approve it, and not squashed when it does. A test that sets the mock helper to approve/deny and then checks root's effective uid on a created file would cover the feature itself.

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The kernel/userspace downcall contract this patch extends is documented with the options and principal fields transposed, in the man page, in the parser's own comment and in the commit message.

On testing: test_300 only checks that `disable_rootsquash` shows up in /proc/mounts. Nothing exercises the part that carries the security weight - that the option is honoured only when the upcall approves it, and that root is still squashed on the dynamic nodemap when it is not. Could sanity-sec add a case that creates a file as root with and without the option and checks the resulting ownership?

(2 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, a couple of comments inline. The one thing that stands out at the whole-patch level is coverage: sanity-sec test_300 only checks that "disable_rootsquash" shows up in the client's mount option string, so nothing exercises what the patch actually adds. Could the test go one step further and assert the server side of it - that the dynamic gssiam nodemap for the connection comes up with admin=1 (lctl get_param nodemap.<hash>.admin) when the upcall approves GSSIAM_OPT_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH, and stays 0 when the option is absent? A root-owned create under the fileset with the uid checked afterwards would cover the end-to-end behaviour. As it stands a regression anywhere between lgmi_options and nodemap_gssiam_attrs_update() would leave test_300 passing.

Patch Set 53:

(9 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The main ones are the unconditional gssiam_managed bypass added to nodemap_set_raise_privs() and the fact that the client-supplied options word is now unvalidated all the way into MDT0's upcall cache key.

The new test only checks that the mount option string is parsed and echoed back by /proc/mounts. Nothing exercises what the patch actually adds on the server: the ":options" downcall field, GSSIAM_AUTH_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH, and the resulting allow_root on the per-identity nodemap. Could sanity-sec grow a case that mounts with disable_rootsquash and then verifies nodemap.<name>.admin_nodemap (and that root is still squashed when the upcall does not approve it)?

Patch Set 54:

(2 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, two small carry-over comments inline.

The new coverage in sanity-sec test_300 only checks that `disable_rootsquash` shows up in the mount option string; nothing exercises what the option actually does. Since the mock l_gssiam_auth always approves the request, an end-to-end case looks feasible: mount a GSSIAM client without the option and confirm root is still squashed, mount again with it and confirm root keeps its identity. That is the part of the patch (downcall options field -> GSSIAM_AUTH_DISABLE_ROOT_SQUASH -> allow_root raise on the child nodemap) that a parsing test cannot catch.
